ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u6	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! Thank you very much for this question, all the more important because the programme My current is very popular and the prosumer movement in Poland is developing. I would like to begin by answering this important question, to inform the High House that according to the Polish Society for Transmission and Distribution of Electricity at the end of March this year. 1006775 micro-installations with a total power of more than 7.3 GW were connected to the electricity grid. In a word, we have achieved not so long ago unimaginable progress in this area - we have exceeded the level of 1 million prosumers in Poland. In comparison, it has to be said that in December 2015 it was around 4 000. Prosumers. The conscious, deliberate policy of the Government of Law and Justice, the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, is aimed at the intensive development of renewable energy sources, the change of the Polish energy mix towards a more sustainable mix, in which there are stable sources, allowing for the supply of the national system of final customers, especially industry, individual consumers, but also institutions, in electricity, but also in which the share of renewable energy sources which allows the greater competitiveness of the Polish economy, especially entrepreneurs, in the export of goods, which can be legitimised in their passports of products with a greater share of green, clean energy. According to the Energy Market Agency at the end of February, the installed capacity of renewable energy sources was more than 18.1 GW and still in December 2015 it was 7 GW, the largest of which are photovoltaic sources - 8.7 GW, and in these photovoltaics as much as 75% , ladies and gentlemen, these are prosumer micro-installations, so-called. Civil energy. In addition, it is worth informing the High House that in 2020 Poland achieved the RES target set by the European Commission. Let me remind you, it was a 15% target. And we've reached 16.1%. So we've even crossed that line. To answer the Member's question, first of all, I would like to say that my electricity programme 4.0 , the new edition, is aimed at prosumers who settle on the basis of a new billing model, so-called. net billing. This is the principle of selling surplus energy, actually values, not the amount of electricity accounted for in zlotys, not in kilowato-hours. The primary objective is to reduce electricity bills among prosumers and produce energy in micro-installations for your own use, i.e. to increase self-consumption, to produce this energy for your household. How do I apply for aid from my electricity 4.0? Through the application generator on the website of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. This is a request generated through the platform: Generator of applications for funding, address: https://gwd.nfosigw.gov.pl, i.e. bookmarks on the website of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management. In total, the maximum amount of aid from this scheme to the installation may amount to PLN 20 500, including up to PLN 5 000. PLN for photovoltaic installation, 7.5 thousand. PLN for home energy storage, lithium-ion storage - mostly this is the technology, 5 thousand. zł for hot water storage, hot water pump and 3 000. PLN for home energy management system. I would particularly like to draw the attention of the High House to this latter element. The home energy management system is an electronic system that allows us to optimize production, but also consumption, energy management in our household, allowing us to program, optimize, increase energy efficiency of our household. In addition to the Clean Air programme, we have been financing the thermomodernisation of single-family buildings for many years, the exchange of heating sources, and the possibility of installing photovoltaic panels. These elements that we offer in today's programme My current 4.0 they make our homes smarter, they will be more energy efficient, they will be more energy-efficient, and above all, they drive prosumers to a new way of acting, namely, as I said earlier, to increase self-consumption, to store energy, to change energy consumption in time when energy is cheaper in these tariffs. You can also choose the right tariff from the energy supplier (the Bell) to make the best use of these technical solutions and reduce the electricity bills that we pay each month by using this energy for our household.

On the other hand, if there are a lot of these installations locally in a given locality, the so-called phenomenon arises during sunny days. energy peaks produced at the peak of this energy production. And even if we built a power system prepared for this maximum value of energy transmission, it would be very expensive, irrational. Therefore, in the programme my electricity 4.0 , but also in the new clearing system so-called. net-billing, which results from the Market Directive and the RED II Directive, which is de facto the implementation of European legislation into national order, we aim to help prosumers increase self-consumption, i.e. to transfer as little as possible of this energy produced in photovoltaic installations in our home to low voltage networks and to consume as much as possible on site. But sometimes we can't use it at a certain time because we're at work, at school, away from home. That is why we want to support the development of energy storage, both electricity storage and heat storage, so that we can store this energy until we get home when we can use it. However, according to the new billing model (the bell) we have 12 months, because we have an individual prosumer account, which we can settle within 12 months. At the end of the last sentence, Mr. Marshal, it is worth to say that the prosumer does not have to make sales transactions. He will not be obliged to conduct business. He will not pay personal income tax in this respect and the energy produced by the prosumer is not subject to excise duty. Therefore, the government will continue to support the development of the Prosumer movement. In the new law, which entered into force on 1 April this year, we also introduced the concepts of collective prosumer and virtual prosumer. We're opening up the prosumer market to actually dozens of millions of households. And there is the prospect of a dynamic development of this sector. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It is able to take 36 million tons of oil per year, which is absolutely sufficient for our operations and for security in terms of the supply of this raw material, oil, from other directions. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u24	Good morning. Dear Marshal, Honourable ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased to present to you information and explanations on the question addressed to the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration on the threat of local investment co-financed by the Polish Government Fund Loads: Strategic Investment Programme due to the question, as put out in the question and as you have put it, the drastic increase in costs, the lack of possibility to value contracts and the value of contracts, the lack of the possibility to adapt the funding criteria to the real situation. First of all, I would like to remind you that the Polish Government Fund: The Strategic Investment Programme was introduced in July 2021 by a resolution of the Council of Ministers. The aim of the programme is primarily to stimulate investment activity of local government units. The programme is implemented by the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego investment promesy, thanks to which it is possible to obtain by local authorities or their associations non-refundable investments which are important from the point of view of local communities. The programme shall be financed by the COVID counter-fund. This programme is of great interest to local governments, which confirms that the decision of the government of Mr Mateusz Morawiecki to create it was appropriate. In this context, it is worth noting that in the first edition of the Government Fund of Poland Loads: the Strategic Investment Program of local governments has contributed almost 8 thousand. applications for funding for a total amount of over PLN 93 billion. The first edition of the programme covered 4040 applications for a total amount of almost PLN 24 billion. According to data collected by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego from applicants, in the first edition of the programme, local authorities have already signed 1016 investment contracts, which is 25% provision of preliminary promenades, for a total amount of PLN 4.7 billion, which in turn represents 20% the budget of the first edition of the programme. Tender procedures are settled on an average of 74% the value of the investments indicated in the application for funding. Seeing the great interest of local governments in the first edition of the programme, and also taking into account the positive impact of the programme on the development of local communities, Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, following the resolutions of the Council of Ministers, decided to launch the next three editions of this programme, which were addressed to local governments. In this respect, I kindly inform you that the second edition, like the first one, is open and covers all local government units and their associations, the third edition is addressed to municipalities, powiats and their associations, in areas in which national agricultural enterprises operated, the fourth edition is addressed to spa communities and have the status of a spa protection area. At this point it is also worth noting that the plan of the COVID-19 Anti-Action Fund for the implementation of the four previous editions of the programme secures funds at the level of PLN 47 billion. By presenting this information, I kindly inform you that the law firm of the President of the Council of Ministers, as well as Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego constantly monitor the implementation of the programme. Any questions raised in this regard and any proposals for change, including from ladies and gentlemen of Members, shall be carefully examined and the programme shall be adapted to the changing circumstances which make it difficult for local governments to implement the investment. In this respect, we see, first and foremost, delays in the settlement of subsequent tender procedures or other difficulties in obtaining contractors by local authorities. In view of these circumstances, on 26 April this year. The Council of Ministers adopted a resolution amending the resolution on the establishment of the Polish Government Fund: Strategic Investment Programme. I would therefore like to inform you that the changes made meet the expectations and demands made by the local authorities, as well as those raised by the Member in her speech. In this respect, I would like to point out that, thanks to the amendment of the resolution of the Council of Ministers, it will be possible: to extend the deadline for the announcement of the purchase procedure from 6 to 9 months after the opening promesa by Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego; to introduce the possibility of re-announcing the purchase procedure or part thereof within 9 months of the opening promesa - such an action should be carried out in compliance with public procurement rules; to extend the deadline for applicants to submit a statement to the investment promesa to 30 working days - so far it was 14 days; to make a statement to the investment promesa by those units of the local government whose promesa expired due to the incorrect submission of this statement. Other postulates also come to us, among others. o: introducing the possibility of automatic increase in the value of the promesa due to inflation and increase in investment costs; changing the rules for the payment of the co-financing from the programme; introducing the possibility of changing the scope of the investment task; allowing the possibility of concluding contracts with the contractors of part of the investment without waiting to settle the remaining parts of the tender. However, given the nature of the programme, in particular its competitive nature and the need to ensure equal treatment of all local authorities applying for support under the programme, and given the scale of the programme and the measures secured for this purpose, it was not possible to introduce all the proposed changes at this stage. I would also like to point out that if any more detailed questions arise, we are, of course, in contact with local authorities - both BGK and KPRM - and at your disposal, so we will also answer any specific questions in writing. Thank you very much.

Thanks to the construction nearly 2,000. km of new high-speed rail lines. The investment process is in Poland - because we are part of the European Union - extremely formalized, extremely regulated by law and from this right comes a whole range of consulting obligations on the investor's side. You will not indicate, because there is no such case, a place where an investor in the form of a CPK company would have failed to fulfil any of the consultation obligations. Conversely, the CPK makes a whole range of efforts, initiatives, consultation meetings which are not required by the law, which result from the investor's goodwill, so that the maximum amount of this major investment project can be consulted in order to take into account all kinds of comments and conclusions as far as possible. It is enough to say that we are already after several stages of such consultations, following strategic consultation of the voivodship, after two stages of consultation of the strategic location study, the programme of the new transport system of the country, after a strategic environmental impact assessment. At the same level as SSL - 160 thousand. Notes. This is the largest programme of consultation of infrastructure investments in Poland in general, in the history of Poland. Never has such a large consultation been carried out as those already carried out, which I stress, the law did not require, are something additional, precisely because of the good will of the investor in this direction. Now we are in the case of 10 sections, nearly 1300 km, at the stage of technical and environmental studies. Again, this is not an investor's will, it is the law that is rooted in EU legislation that results from this procedure. In open procedures, in accordance with the Public Procurement Law, the entities that carry out these analyses have been designated. These are technical and environmental studies, i.e. for different variants, many variants, an analysis of technical, economic and environmental aspects within the meaning of both the environment and the social environment. Meetings are held - again beyond what is required, because public participation at this stage is not legally required. There's a whole series of meetings going on, first online meetings, because there was a pandemic, then personal meetings, then live. For the Silesian episode itself, this means at least 25 meetings that have already taken place are planned. Of course, the demands and comments of both residents and local authorities are taken into account and are being considered. Perhaps sometimes the impression that this is not the case may result from the fact that there are proposals which are simply obviously impossible to implement for objective reasons, e.g. a proposal for progress which leads through areas subject to such environmental protection levels that no investment can be made here. After finding such a fact, it makes no sense to further analyze the course carried out, because it is simply by law that this course cannot be conducted. In the case of Silesia we have a number of factors which make this maneuvering area narrow unfortunately. In some places there is a phenomenon, you can say, so-called. narrow throat, that is, different variants, different runs must pass about in a given place and there is no room for manoeuvre for objective reasons. I've already mentioned protected areas. The areas of mining damage and active mining activity also significantly reduce investment opportunities. We're here, on this episode. We are implementing the EU corridor in the TEN-T network, so we cannot go beyond the parameters of this corridor and beyond the range of this corridor. This is a cross-border investment, agreed with the Czech side, so of course we must aim at this point at the border, to which the Czech side will join. This also causes that any possible mileage, variants are out. Finally, each rail - especially in the case of high-speed rail - has to meet certain technical parameters. There can be arches, more narrow, with a turning beam exceeding certain parameters, there can be driveways, exits exceeding certain parameters. This also immediately eliminates a whole range of other ways, perhaps more socially acceptable, because they are technically impossible. This means that the implementation of them would result in the project not meeting the assumed parameters. Finally, the next case may be those routes which significantly increase the length of the railway line, because in general, how to build transport links between cities And the city of B, it must be somewhere in the vicinity of a straight line connecting these points, not to meander or perform double arches or triple lengths. All this causes that some proposed options are quite quickly rejected, after a short analysis, while all of them are analysed. The feeling that they are not being analyzed is, of course, wrong. We are now at the second stage of our work, it is a technical and environmental study. It will end with the choice of an investor option (The Bell) and at least two alternative options to be submitted as part of the application for an environmental decision. The environmental decision procedure is once again expected to involve the public legally, requests are made before an investor independent body, i.e. the regional environmental directorate, and there is still a judicial route, a reference to the environmental decision. All these rules, all these procedures that we apply, make the participation of the public, the participation of local authorities in this investment process multifaceted, multi-level and, where possible, these proposals will be, as far as technology allows, as far as environmental protection allows, so far as economic rationality is concerned, taken into account. Dear Ladies and Gentlemen, The exception to the ban on flights of aircraft and carriers of the Russian Federation provides for a regulation of the European Union. This is Regulation No 2022/334 of the European Union of 28 February 2022 amending Council Regulation No 883/2014 concerning restrictive measures in view of Russia's actions destabilising the situation in Ukraine on the basis of which the flight of aircraft and air carriers of the Russian Federation in the European Union was prohibited. This regulation provides for exceptions to this prohibition. Such exceptions shall be those of humanitarian status and of the following status: emergency condition, return flights of rented aircraft for which the consent of the relevant European aviation authorities has been given, and emergency landing or emergency flight. Let me remind you once again: the consent of the relevant European aviation authorities has been given. Pursuant to the Regulation, competent authorities may authorise the landing of a Russian aircraft in the territory of the European Union, take-off or flight over the territory, provided that those competent authorities have previously stated that such landing, take-off or flight is necessary for humanitarian reasons or any other reason compatible with the objectives of this Regulation. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u226	I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 20 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on support and rehabilitation of minors (print No 2183). I kindly ask the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Michał Wosi to present the reasons for the bill. Come in, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u227	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the fruit of several years of work of the Ministry of Justice and the expert team under the direction of Professor Henryk Haak, whom I also thank for this hard work. This is a reform project related to the rehabilitation of minors, reform of the entire system of rehabilitation of minors. It is a comprehensive law, a comprehensive reform in both procedural and institutional terms. It is a very extensive law, in fact, about the size of codes, because it contains more than 400 articles. At a certain stage of the work, there were even considerations as to whether a certain codec tradition should be restored from 1932 and whether the current juvenile law is simply an obsolete act from 4 decades ago, a law which was introduced during the time of martial law and was in line with the needs of that period. Of course, it has been revised during these 40 years, while a whole range of legal institutions can already be considered obsolete. As part of this comprehensive reform, we propose a whole range of changes and new institutions. This is primarily to take account of the new social reality and the change in the nature of juvenile offences. Data clearly indicate that there is a brutalisation of these acts, that there are more sexual acts, as well as acts related to drug crime. In the draft law, we want to introduce more effective measures for the impact and support of the rehabilitation process for minors, which is appropriate above all to the degree of their demoralisation. It can be said that the project itself introduces such a clear philosophy of action, namely it stops the state machine at the right level against the slightest transgressions. That's the right level for the principal of the school, the police. You don't have to run the whole forensic machine. At the same time, the project introduces an effective rehabilitation of those who committed the most serious criminal offences. As part of the bill, the regulations that have not lost their validity are incorporated from the Juvenile Procedure Act. The bill strengthens the procedural guarantees of minors as well as executes judgments of the European Court of Human Rights, especially in the case of Grabowski against Poland. Within the first block, i.e. reactions to minors who commit the most serious criminal acts, the new institutions that are being introduced are, above all, mandatory inclusion in the correctional facility, the need to place minors who have committed the most serious crimes, as defined in Article. 10 § 2 The Penal Code, which is the so-called "Criminal Code." relative obligation. At present, the system operates in such a way that, in any case, the court considers the possibilities from the softest to the most serious measure, while in the case of these most brutal crimes, we change the philosophy of action, namely the court will first consider the juvie, and only when it decides on the corrective measure and sees that other measures can be more effective, then it takes such a decision and must justify it. The project proposes two institutions related to these most brutal juvenile offences, two new institutions, i.e. the possibility of threatening the minor to apply a corrective measure up to the age of 24 or immediately extending the implementation of the corrective measure up to the age of 24. This occurs in particularly justified cases where there is a very high degree of corruption, a kind of criminal act, and the manner and circumstances of committing it. We introduce the possibility for a family court to rule on the obligation of a minor to be released from correction facilities for specific proceedings or on the supervision of a probation officer. It's a novelty, it's not in the current system. The point is that, when a minor is released from a correctional facility most often at the age of 21 or faster, if someone is released from the correction facility sooner, the state actually loses any knowledge of such a minor, and perhaps it is that he should continue therapy or the curator should continue to support him if this rehabilitation process in the establishment was not going on as it should have been. The Act introduces a new educational measure. At the moment, there are a dozen educational measures and a correctional measure. As part of the comprehensive project submitted, there will be 11 educational measures and 12 corrective measures. This new educational measure, i.e. the placement in the district educational centre, has been advocated for years by professionals, by the community of family judges, also by specialists involved in rehabilitation, working with minors, because practice shows that in this current catalog there is no educational remedy that would be appropriate for minors who commit criminal acts, but the degree of their demoralization does not support the use of a correctional measure or does not give rise to the inclusion in the correctional facility. First of all, the aim was to improve the effectiveness of rehabilitation in youth centres themselves. It can be said that MOWs, as the most serious educational measure, have turned into places where minors are directed to one centre, who for some reason, after committing a criminal offence, have not been sent to a correctional facility, e.g. After the murder, but at the same time in the same MOW, there are children depraved, even though some notorious dwarfs are staying in the same center, which obviously makes it difficult to rehabilitate one and the other, so the more serious one can be said of another security, the educational measure is necessary. The fact that the youth educational centers were not doing so well is evidenced by the tragic events in Lańcut, Wałbrzych in 2020, the murder of two teenagers, and other minor cases that are there every day, need not be mentioned. The special distress, the plague in MOWs, is, of course, the escapes that occur on a global scale, in thousands. So the new measure is also intended to address this issue, it is intended to be an instrument that will make it possible for minors who have committed a criminal act to be placed in the district educational centre, but who have not been sent to a correctional institution, but also those who are staying in the educational centre, but for some reason this rehabilitation is ineffective there. Therefore, they will be able to be placed in this district educational centre. Under the Act there is a whole package related to protection for the youngest. First of all, we define the lower age limit for the demoralization and commissioning of this judicial machine, at the level of 10 years, it is the responsibility of minors for demoralization. At the moment, there is no lower age limit in terms of responsibility, so it has happened that 6-, 7-year-old children have been brought to justice and, after all, to such children who are not aware have no sense of responsibility for their actions, the family code is appropriate and caring. The measures are provided for parents, not minors, as to whether there is a minor-led procedure. We are introducing a new institution, a new institution in the legal system, namely the application to minors by the school head or by executives, the police above all, of educational influence. With these measures, it will not be necessary to notify the family court of these various minor cases. It's about the principal of the school, instead of bringing the case to court, when it goes on, there's a whole trial, he could have ordered it right away, for example. rake leaves in the autumn or wash the corridor, or apply other possibilities which the law provides for precisely for those directors who can use these legal tools provided for by the law on a daily basis. Finally, it is also possible to oblige parents or underage carers to cooperate in the youth center of sociotherapy. Another important amendment under the Act is the introduction of homes for mother and child. At the moment, there is no legal basis for having minors with their child in MOWs or maybe in educational centres or in correction facilities, if they do not threaten the child in any way. This is more stringent than in the case of prisons, where women who are deprived of freedom take direct care of this child until the third year of age of the child, if, of course, safety concerns are involved. So we also introduce the possibility of running such homes for mothers and children for minors who gave birth as part of the rehabilitation system of minors, under the care of the state. We are also introducing, and this is an important change, the Juvenile Medication Commission. At the moment, courts may decide whether or not to oblige a minor to take various therapies and medical measures, but after the decision of such a medical remedy, the minor's family may sometimes be left alone in this case, i.e. the court is not in a position to find a centre which executes the judgment of the court. There'll be full coordination right now. The Commission led by the Minister of Health, appointed by the Minister of Health, will coordinate the placement of minors in those places where they require treatment. Finally, we have a whole package of legal guarantees for minors in the context of the proceedings. The aim is to extend the catalogue of indications regarding the appointment of a public defender in the field of compulsory defence. It's going to be a bigger shot than it is now. We also have here an instruction on the right of defence, which at the moment does not exist, the mandatory involvement of the defender, the prosecutor in the most serious cases carried out in the context of proceedings under the bill submitted, the project submitted, the whole package related to the possibility of casatorial actions, i.e. the possibility of filing a complaint for detention, for personal inspection, also for the use of means of direct coercion, and a comprehensive definition of the rules for carrying out personal checks, where at the moment these rules are not recognised, and certainly not at the statutory level. Finally, the whole package related to the application procedure, the extension of provisional measures. This is the implementation of Grabowski's ruling against Poland, i.e. the definition of general conditions for interim measures. It is about shelters for minors, introducing a family court order for a period of not more than 3 months. This period of stay shall be fixed and this shall also be subject to appeal. It can be said that the last new institution to be set up under this law is a committee for the directing of minors to a youth educational centre in order to improve coordination, because this coordination is, of course, at the level of the correction facilities for which the Minister of Justice is responsible at the moment. The Commission is directing the most suitable centre in this case to the most effective rehabilitation process. In contrast, youth educational centers are often run by local authorities, but supervised by the curatoria, and the head of the ORE is run by the Minister of National Education, which is why there is a great deal of confusion about this and hence the whole package of changes ordering these issues. Ladies and gentlemen! The proposed project will certainly improve the process of rehabilitation of minors. It is a very good bill drawn up by a group of experts who worked for a few years, a few years on this deep, comprehensive reform. From the perspective of the Ministry of Justice, procedural issues are very important, because it is, it is possible to say, precisely such a juvenile code related to judicial procedures, but also political, organisational issues which define how to implement corrective measures, how to implement educational measures, and order the whole system, primarily strengthen the effectiveness of the state towards the most demoralized minors. Always minors have no criminal responsibility and this is extremely important, it is the state that is trying to do so. If other actions have failed before, if the family has failed or different care institutions, the state takes on the responsibility of bringing young, angry people straight. In this way, the whole package of effective tools will certainly improve the level of rehabilitation for which the State is responsible. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

It has been almost 40 years since the Juvenile Procedure Act was passed. We are talking about the Law of 26 October 1982 on juvenile proceedings. While an attempt was made in 2013 to amend this law, many issues still required legal regulation or clarification, which was repeatedly advocated by practitioners. This proposal, Minister, your project is trying to make amends. Indeed, the long-term work under your direction, Minister, in the Ministry of Justice, brings in the guesthouse a draft law which organizes and systematizes the matter of juvenile cases, refers to the constitutional and international standards in force in Poland, brings to the bill's rank certain solutions which have so far been regulated in lower-rank legal acts. In addition, it introduces a number of new, challenging proposals and impacts to strengthen their efficiency. Secondly, the substance of this solution is already indicated in the title itself: the act on support and rehabilitation of minors. It includes in the holistic perspective a person who is in the process of becoming in different dimensions of development and experiencing sometimes undesirable and reprehensible episodes related to violations of criminal or social standards, which necessarily requires response and attempts to correct, repair. It therefore contains provisions of both procedural and material and implementing nature, which are synergistic and complementary to each other. The whole is embedded in the belief that there is an effective power of intelligent support in order to shape or rebuild the desirable and socially acceptable attitudes of minors. In the design of the authors, the project has the advantage of the possibility of individualized paths of support, correction, rehabilitation, which results from a wide range of methods of impact at different stages of the process of support and rehabilitation. Thirdly, the extensive, detailed matter of the bill brings a new breath in the process of dealing with minors as a whole. E.g. specify the lower age threshold for an individual to whom corruption proceedings may be initiated - the Minister indicated in his submission the age of 10 years. In the case of criminal acts, legislators leave the lower limit of underage age 13 years. Such action shall be conditional upon the Director's acceptance of this solution by parents or legal guardians and minors themselves. The repertoire of possible educational measures includes instruction, oral or written warning, apology to the victim, restoration of the previous condition and the execution of specific work for the school. At the same time, it is valuable to draw attention to the possibility of mobilising parents and guardians in their educational and caring activities through the possibility of punishing them in the event of a breach of the obligations imposed by the court. Another desirable proposal is the whole package of solutions for exploratory behaviour, including. concerning the parties to proceedings and the victim, including their rights and obligations in those proceedings. The provisions on detention and provisional measures have been laid down and the provisions on the conduct of the primary and the appeal proceedings have been clarified, which logically introduces us to the enforcement procedure, making it equivalent in the provisions of this law to the exploratory procedure. These rules fully and completely underline the importance of this procedure in the work of the family court and its greater codification than before. The bill is to replace the existing rules adopted more than 40 years ago. Minors due to their age, lack of experience or pathological family environment are particularly vulnerable to various types of abuse. It is therefore important to create an appropriate legal framework that respects their respective rights and strengthens their protection. Moreover, it should be borne in mind that the handling of juvenile cases should not be restrictive, but focus primarily on upbringing. Criminal policy against minors does not provide any positive solutions. In the case of minors, it is important to move away from repressive solutions to care solutions. The controlling-restrictive climate is the least beneficial for the process of rehabilitation of minors, and I have the impression that this project is going a little bit in this direction. It should be borne in mind that an important element of effective rehabilitation is the creation of solutions that will eliminate not only the effects but also the causes of pathological behaviour. Effective mechanisms must be created, allowing for simultaneous impact and on minors, and on the family environment, because even if the minor is successfully rehabilitated and he returns to the family environment affected by the same problems that caused his demoralization, then there are great opportunities for a renewed conflict between minors and the law. Furthermore, the issue of support and assistance for former school pupils in the process of their return to normal life is very important. This return is difficult and without proper support, it is likely that such a child will again be in conflict with the law. These are issues where regulation would improve the effectiveness of the impact on minors. High Izbo! The processed project introduces many new solutions. Among the important ones, as has already been said here, we can include changes, for example. the establishment of a lower age limit of 10 years from which minors will be able to respond in cases of demoralisation. The projector introduces a new educational measure in the form of placing a minor in a district educational centre. There will be room for young people who do not yet show a high degree of demoralization. In addition, the project provides for the possibility for the school principal to impose certain obligations on a minor who shows signs of depravity at the school or who has committed a criminal offence. A novelty in the project is the possibility of deciding to carry out a corrective measure up to the age of 24, i.e. we may have a situation when a person who has completed 13. One year old and committed a certain act, spent 11 years in an establishment. Some experts point out that there is a very high risk of secondary demoralization after such a long period of stay at the plant. It is also highly likely that such a person will not be able to function effectively in society in accordance with its standards and will most likely return to the criminal path. Unacceptable are proposals that offer the possibility of preventive use of handcuffs by district staff of educational centres, establishments or shelters. The Ombudsman pointed out that the use of handcuffs is not appropriate in the case of minors who are under the care of underage staff due to: firstly, their stigmatising nature, secondly, the different objectives of juvenile facilities in relation to prisons and arrests, and thirdly, the higher risk in the case of tight fastening used during transport. In addition, there are fears that the preventive application of a direct coercion measure should not become a rule. Another issue is the use of a safety straitjacket, a restraint belt, a safety helmet. It seems that these measures should also not be applied in juvenile facilities because of their oppressive, stigmatizing and degrading nature. As experts point out, the use of this measure constitutes a disproportionate use of force. The possibility of placing a minor in the adaptation chamber is a great concern. There is a great deal of room for abuse here and this poses a direct risk of using the adaptation chamber as an informal disciplinary measure resulting in isolation. We must remember that any form of isolation of minors is a measure that has a negative impact on the physical and mental state of the child. There are a lot of records here that raise doubts, which does not change the fact that these are very necessary solutions that need to be worked out reliably. That is why we are in favour of directing the project to further work. Of course, our support or not for the project is linked to the course of this work. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u238	Thank you kindly, Member. Mr Jacek Protasiewicz, on behalf of the Polish Coalition. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u239	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Polish Coalition Club, I intend to present our position on the government draft law on support and rehabilitation of minors. The project deals with a comprehensive regulation of issues relating to minors whose behaviour requires the intervention of state authorities. The proposals contained in this document concern, inter alia,: the creation of new juvenile facilities in the form of district educational centres, the introduction of the possibility of out-of-court response to the proper behaviour of minors, the establishment of a minimum age limit for demoralisation at the level of 10 years. Undoubtedly, it is important to appreciate the inclusion of all offences in the concept of criminal conduct, not just strictly defined, as is currently the case. Often carried out on a regular basis, minor offences appear to indicate a higher degree of demoralization than committing a destructive act of crime. We also consider the possibility, with the consent of the parents, of imposing certain duties on a minor by the school's principal, who exhibits depravity or criminal acts on the school premises. The inclusion of parents in this process through their consent is also part of the pedagogy of adult carers or parents themselves. It is doubtful to leave without changing the catalogue of depraved behaviors. We agree with the justification of the project that over the years in practice this catalogue has already been defined and accepted. Nor does it raise much doubt, but it is worth noting that the records, which contain the indicated manifestations of the demoralization of minors, were created a long time ago in the 1970s, so today they need to be given new content, as well as new manifestations resulting from social transformations, such as those related to the Internet or the much more widespread availability of drugs. It is also worth more appreciating the educational measure in the form of a referral to the probation centre. It is a good, effective institution, which has not yet been sufficiently appreciated. The courts refer minors to these facilities to a small extent, because there are relatively few of them, and they should be set up at each regional court, have clearly defined tasks and funding. They should be financed by those courts as well as have more powers, especially in the case of managers of centres and their disposal of funds at their disposal. On the other hand, we consider it dangerous to think about the possibility of extending the reform measure until the age of 24. This is a kind of way out to meet the expectations of sometimes demanding a very strict punishment of minors who commit violent crimes of the public, but the conditions for extending their stay to the age of 24 are, I would say, judged. The danger lies in the fact that, on average, after 3 years in a correctional facility, there is a high risk of secondary demoralization, so that the rehabilitation process does not last for more than 3 years. It is hard to imagine that a person who is 13 years of age and who has committed a criminal act would remain in a correctional institution for 11 years until a very mature age, or 24 years of age. After leaving the facility, it will be a completely dysfunctional person, deprived of any adaptability and will end up in a penitentiary sooner than expected by adapting to society. High Seym! However, this project does not address the three most important problems which would improve the effectiveness of the impact on juvenile offenders of criminal acts or depravity. First of all, existing and proposed solutions provide for interference in the educational process of minors only when they show depravity. This means that the state's response to dangerous symptoms in the behaviour of minors is very often and will be late. The current system most often sees a minor when he is already on the road to crime or is already heavily addicted to alcohol or drugs. Yet symptoms appear here much earlier and following this dangerous path should be stopped at a much earlier stage rather than reacting when it is too late. The second serious negligence that has been waiting for nearly 40 years to be made up is the creation of mechanisms for simultaneous impact on minors and the family environment. For many years it has been obvious, it is written by literature, and experts say that the family is the most important educational environment for the child. Unfortunately, this is not much in practice. There are still no systemic solutions in the area of so-called. educating the family. If we want to effectively rehabilitate young people, we must also help older ones: parents or carers. The third problem is the real help and support of former school pupils locked up in the process of their return to normal life. It is important to be aware that many of them have been rehabilitating since early childhood. Even a parent who first goes to such a facility and leaves as an adult is colliding with problems he didn't know as a child. Often these people are merely helpless, because today's system teaches their claims rather than their responsibilities and creativity. They have problems in solving current problems, in re-entering the family environment. Today there are no systemic solutions supporting them in this difficult process of social adaptation. It would be good if the proposed law on this topic and this issue would also be taken to the workshop. As far as these three fundamental problems are concerned, they have not been resolved by the existing legal status, nor has the draft law responded to them in any way. Despite these shortcomings, many of the proposals contained in the project are worth attention, which is why the Polish Coalition Club, which I represent here, i.e. the PSL, the Union of European Democrats and Conservatives, requests that the project be submitted to the committee for further work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u316	You will also answer the rapporteur, Minister. State Secretary at the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage Jarosław Sellin: Good. First of all, I wanted to express my gratitude to the group of twenty... As I read in the document, 24 Members signed the bill and submitted it. It's good timing and good sense of need. The point is to change the name of this center and its course of action. So that I express my gratitude for this initiative. Indeed, the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage oversees the activities of the Polish-Russian Centre for Dialogue and Understanding for 11 years. We have assessed the projects undertaken by this public institution as important, interesting, efficiently implemented and supporting the Polish state's right, but since the Russian aggression against Ukraine in 2014, resulting in the occupation of Crimea and parts of the Donetsk and Lugansk circuit areas, clearly and repeatedly condemned by the centre, multilateral projects involving Ukrainians and Belarusians have played an increasingly important role in the activities of this institution, as well as projects aimed at disseminating reliable knowledge of Russia and its policies in Poland and beyond. The Centre rejected Russian interlocutors' demands to focus solely on bilateral actions, believing that any Polish-Russian dialogue could not abstract Russian policy against common neighbours. The growing dislike of the Russian public institutions to have a serious conversation led the centre to devote more attention to the Russian opposition environments publicly opposed to aggressive foreign policy and to suppressing civil liberties and freedoms in Russia. In addition, the Centre, in agreement with the Ministry, implemented numerous projects aimed at Ukrainian society. It is worth noting, for example, the launch of an Internet magazine mentioned hereNew Poland in Ukrainian or public debates on international law organised in agreement with the Ministry of Foreign Affairs of Ukraine in Kiev, in Lviv, Odessa, combined with a presentation published by the Center of the book "The Case of Crimes Annexation under International Lawż." The Ministry noted the problems associated with the institution's taking action, especially in Ukraine, under the current name: the Centre for Polish-Russian Dialogue and Understanding. Of course, it raised reservations among some Poles, and all the more it generated understandable emotions in Ukrainians and sometimes Belarusians or other nations. On the other hand, this name and the current mandate of the institutions facilitated the acquisition of contacts by the Russian Centre, and also constituted a skillful instrument used by the Polish diplomacy among the countries of the European Union and NATO to reject allegations made by the Russian Federation of alleged Polish Rusofobia. Everything changed in the morning on February 24th. - Russia's invasion of Ukraine. It has brought about a fundamental change in the circumstances of the action. In the strongest possible words, the Centre condemned the Russian state's crime of aggression, stressing in its statement that the necessary condition for dialogue with the Russians was their clear recognition of the rights of other nations to sovereignty, territorial integrity and democracy, and their exclusion from war and condemnation by the Russian army of war crimes. The Ministry of Culture believes that Russia's war against Ukraine forces the urgent transformation of the Centre of Polish-Russian Dialogue and Agreement, as well as the change of the name of the institution itself, so that the centre can take action corresponding to the new reality. The name so far creates a polarly different association from the actual role of the institution and from its actions, and makes it extremely difficult for Ukraine to provide assistance. Moreover, the further distinction only of Russia among the eastern neighbours of the Republic of Poland as a country with which the dialogue enjoys the support of the Polish state in the form of a special institution is now perceived as unwarranted advantages of this country, and this is in a situation where they violate the basic standards of international law. The organisation of dialogue with representatives of the Russian democratic environments is, of course, in line with the Polish state's rationale, but it should not obscure today's major objectives: to support the Ukrainian people and the Ukrainian state in the fight against the aggressor and the Belarusian people in the fight for democracy. For these reasons, the Ministry is solidarity on the urgent need to adopt the amendment contained in the submitted sejm printing. We believe that the transformation of the Centre for Polish-Russian Dialogue and Understanding into the Centre for Dialogue. Juliusz Mieroszewski will quickly launch a wide range of tools for new tasks, supporting Ukraine and the Ukrainians and Belarusian democratic environments, and will provide a clear political signal sent to Russia and to the Russians, highlighting the fundamental change of circumstances and highlighting the real hierarchy of Polish priorities in Eastern politics. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u319	Mrs. Marshal! Mr. Messengers! Gentlemen! I have the honour to submit to the High House a report on the activities of the State Labour Inspection in 2020. For years, the State Labour Inspection has sought to protect the workers and ensure their safe and lawful employment conditions. However, 2020 was a year of pandemic challenges, which the State Labour Inspection also had to undertake to pursue its core mission, but also to become an important element of the state in a system to reduce the level of risk of SARS-CoV-2 infection in workplaces and all other places where workers can be exposed to coravirus infection. In 2020 labour inspectors carried out a total of over 56 thousand. control. Some of them were implemented in order to ensure adequate health and safety at work for workers during the pandemic. In this respect, we have checked almost 16 thousand. operators and almost half of them inspectors have found irregularities. Our actions in reducing the effects of SARS-CoV-2 concerned the organisation of workplaces, the provision of personal protective equipment and the proper implementation of remote work solutions. Due to the limited competence to issue legal remedies in the event of non-compliance with the requirements to reduce exposure to virus infection, in 462 cases, we informed the competent authorities of the State Sanitary Inspection. It is worth noting that employers aware of the risks posed by coronavirus infection have mostly equipped workers with appropriate protection measures to combat the epidemic. Since mid-March 2020, schools have mainly been teaching in a remote formula, so the return of pupils to schools since 1 September of this year has put a new challenge before the State Labour Inspection. The aim was to ensure safe and hygienic conditions for school stays, including reducing the risk of coravirus infection to a minimum, which is why we launched a school prevention programmeSafely, which supported employers in identifying and reducing hazardous, harmful and burdensome factors in the working environment. During the implementation of this programme, the labour inspectors carried out nearly 6 000 in schools. training for 49,000 education workers. Inspectors cared about the quality and proper parameters of respiratory protection products, i.e. masks, which were still recently in widespread use. At the request of the National Tax Administration, labour inspectors issued opinions on the fulfilment or non-compliance of the essential requirements of various types of personal protective equipment. Only in customs clearance places did the National Labour Inspection inspectors assess 385 products. In total, they issued as many as 295 negative opinions, thus holding back the entry to the Polish and European markets of products and products not meeting the requirements set out in the EU directives. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Nearly 35% of the controls carried out in 2020 were initiated as a result of the complaints being received. They mainly concerned issues related to the non-payment of wages and other benefits resulting from the employment relationship. A large part of them involved irregularities resulting from the employment relationship, as well as problems of working time and working conditions and the legality of employment. Of course, it's close to 50,000. complaints are bothering us because there was too much to report when it comes to not respecting employees' rights. Their number was influenced, among others. increasing awareness of citizens' rights and obligations. Importantly, such awareness also grew among foreigners coming to Poland for commercial purposes. The fact that labour inspectors found nearly 37 000 in 2020 was an example of the irregularities revealed. Indictments, they've issued 160 health and safety decisions, nearly 4,000. wage decisions, 9,000. Commands and nearly 160,000. applications and submissions. It is also worth noting that the actual reduction in control activities resulting from the pandemic has allowed us to focus our actions on other areas. I mean broadly understood counseling conducted by employees of the State Labour Inspection. In 2020 our employees gave a total of over 533 thousand. Legal and technical advice has been provided, with nearly 220 000 during the control activities. tips. Ladies and gentlemen! As far as controls carried out in 2020 are concerned, as much as 11 000. of which were found to be in breach of the provisions on the payment of salaries and other benefits resulting from the employment relationship. Inspectors spent almost 4,000. decisions ordering the payment of remuneration for 29 000. employees. The amount of remuneration recovered exceeded PLN 78 million. In 2020 we carried out 857 checks on compliance with working time rules. They showed a similar pattern as in the previous year. The scale of non-compliance with the 5-day working week principle remained worrying. This type of irregularity was found in every fifth check. In addition, labour inspectors carried out checks on compliance with the prohibition of civil law contracts under conditions indicating the existence of employment relationships. We've checked nearly 25,000 in total. civil law agreements. I'm talking about both contract contracts and contract contracts. More than 2,000 have been questioned. agreements, but I must point out here that sometimes people employed under civil law contracts were not interested in changing the basis for their employment. The objective of the State Labour Inspection in 2020 was also to monitor employers' compliance with restrictions on commercial activities on Sundays and holidays. The fact that we have not identified a single infringement in large-area establishments during the reporting period should be considered a success. However, a significant problem was the use by smaller trading facilities of the provisions of the Act on the restriction of trade on Sundays and holidays and the operation on those days against the intentions of the legislator. This phenomenon increased in 2021, when large-area networks also started using similar solutions. This has led to the need to amend the law which entered into force on 1 February 2022 and has largely resolved this issue. Providing workers with safe, hygienic working conditions is part of every check. The inspection has resulted in the elimination of direct life and health threats for more than 38 000. persons. Measurements of health-related factors were carried out at locations where nearly 20,000 people worked. persons. We have eliminated irregularities in personal protective equipment. The right funds were received from employers almost 18 thousand. persons. As a result of the implementation of our decisions for 240 thousand. persons have been properly assessed for occupational risks. The intervention of labour inspectors also provided 33 thousand. staff carrying out health and safety training. Unfortunately, often failure to comply with health and safety rules ended in 2020 with accidents. Inspectors examined the circumstances and causes of as many as 1755 accidents that killed 217 people. The analysis of the audit data shows, as in previous years, that the two most vulnerable industries are construction and processing. In the reporting year we carried out almost 3,000. control on construction sites, mainly during the construction of new buildings and during renovations. I must stress that irregularities were found in 91% control of operators in the industry. The most irregularities occurred at the stage of organizing construction works, earthworks and above all work at height. It is still an extremely important area of activity of the State Labour Inspection to disclose cases of illegal work, both in the case of Polish citizens and foreigners. In 2020, almost 67 000 checks on the legality of employment were carried out. Polish citizens. There's been an illegal employment of almost 9,000. persons. That's 13%. of the public covered by the checks. In 2020 we also carried out more than 4 000. checks on the legality of employment 19 000 foreigners working in our country. The allocation of illegal work was found to be close to 15% controlled aliens. Most illegally employed foreigners, close to 85%, came from Ukraine. In the context of checks on the legality of employment, the state of compliance with labour law rules vis-à-vis foreigners was also checked. The most irregularities concerned the lack of required medical examinations and health and safety training, non-payment of salaries, non-compliance with working time rules. These are very important facts, especially in the context of the current events in Ukraine and the acceptance by Poland of millions of citizens of this country fleeing the war. Many of them will or have already taken up a job in our country. We must do everything we can not to make them victims of exploitation. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u321	Mr. Marshal! Madam Chief Inspector! High Izbo! Position of the Labour Protection Council on the activities of the State Labour Inspection in 2020 The Labour Protection Council at its meeting on 20 July 2021 took note of the report on the activities of the State Labour Inspection in 2020. The document was presented by the chief labour inspector Katarzyna Łażewska-Hrycko and her deputy Jarosław Leśniewski and Dariusz Mińkowski. During the reporting year 56 000 were carried out by labour inspectors. control at 48,000. entities employing a total of 3 million people. Nearly 35% of the checks carried out were so-called. complaints checks to verify the validity of employee complaints affecting labour inspections. These were mainly related to problems with the payment of wages and other employment benefits, irregularities in working time, working conditions and safety, employment relationships and the legality of employment. In addition, the labour inspection was carried out, among others. checks on the implementation of the obligations to prevent the spread of Coronavirus and the measures taken by the workplace to ensure an adequate level of health and safety at work during the epidemic. The State Labour Inspection also examined the correctness of the conclusion of civil law contracts and fixed-term contracts. The implementation of the obligation to pay a minimum wage for persons working under civil law contracts, compliance with the rules on restrictions on trade on Sundays and holidays and other days, the legality of employment of Polish citizens and foreigners and the correctness of the functioning of the employment agencies were checked. In addition, the labour inspection carried out comprehensive checks on labour law compliance, including health and safety at work. in construction, which has been subject to increased supervision by the State Labour Inspection for years. Inspectors also examined the circumstances and causes of 1755 accidents at work - including 138 collective accidents - in which 217 people were killed. Construction and industrial processing dominate among the most accidental industries. Last year's audits revealed 36,5,000. misdemeanors against employee rights. Inspectors spent over 160,000. health and safety decisions, nearly 4 000. wage decisions, about 9,000. Commands and 157,000. applications included in the speeches. This appropriation is The State Labour Inspection carried out 768 so-called. the first checks, i.e. typical preventive checks, carried out on an audit basis and on an assumption, unless gross irregularities are detected, of non-sanctioned checks. In 2020, the first audit strategy included previously uncontrolled micro-enterprises employing up to nine employees and small establishments employing between 10 and 49 employees who operate on the market for no more than 1 year. In almost all cases, inspectors have found various types of irregularities which force the application of appropriate legal measures in the form of decisions, requests in speeches or orders. As a result of last year's inspection and supervision activities of the State Labour Inspection, more than 30,000. employees received outstanding salaries and other benefits from the employment relationship for a total amount of nearly PLN 43 million, and 456 persons working under a contract or a contract for the provision of services, for which the minimum hourly rate provisions were infringed, received claims for an amount of 698 000. zł. It's been made up of 7.5. thousands. employment contracts with persons who have previously worked under civil law agreements or without a written agreement. A direct threat to life or health has also been eliminated in relation to nearly 40,000. persons. In parallel with the control and supervision activities of the State Labour Inspection, the State Labour Inspection carried out a multi-directional preventive and promotional activity, including inter alia. prevention campaigns, competitions aimed at a maximum number of people providing work, as well as employers responsible for ensuring safe and hygienic working conditions for workers. The labour inspection also carried out extensive counselling activities, including through the PIP Advisory Centre, providing over 533 000 during the reporting year. legal and technical advice. Their subject matter hasn't changed in years. The greatest interest concerns counseling on employment relations, wages and working time. The Labour Protection Council highly assesses the control and surveillance activities and preventive activities of the State Labour Inspection conducted in 2020, difficult in the context of the COVID-19 pandemic. It is all the more important to appreciate the commitment and determination of labour inspectors in enforcing labour law rules, in order to improve safety standards, health and health at work. The Council considers it appropriate to consider the possibilities proposed by the State Labour Inspection for changes to Labour Law, which will enable inspectors to carry out controls more effectively and eliminate bureaucratic barriers to their operation. Meeting the challenges of today's labour market requires employment in the labour inspection of highly qualified inspectors, especially with higher technical education. However, the level of remuneration offered by the PIP is the main barrier to acquiring new workers. The Council was concerned about the state of employment and wages in labour inspections, which are significantly lower than the average salary in the country. The Council notes that the continuation of this situation with the increasing number of new tasks each year may lead to the collapse of the offices due to the departure of workers and the lack of possibility of acquiring new ones. The Council is in favour of strengthening the staff and financial capacity of the State Labour Inspection. Real wage increases and an increase in the number of labour inspectors will allow the State Labour Inspection to adequately respond to the current and future challenges of new technologies and forms of labour supply. The Labour Protection Council welcomes the report on the activities of the State Labour Inspection in 2020 and recommends its adoption to the Sejm. He signed the Chairman of the Labour Protection Council. I would also like to add my sincere thanks to this report for the entire staff of the State Labour Inspection with its leadership and submit it to the Minister, the Chief Labour Inspector of Łażewska-Hrycko. Thank you very much for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u367	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you very much for that. Of course, we are struggling with the recruitment of human resources. I don't think we're the only state institution that has a problem with that. We need financial reinforcement, especially in terms of recruitment of technical education workers. We have also proposed some legislative solutions. I hope that they will meet with your positive response in a short time. On the other hand, in more detail on the questions that have been raised here, I have actually chosen a topic that appears in most of the statements of the country that spoke, namely how the State Labour Inspection prepared for the influx of refugees from Ukraine and their impact on the labour market in Poland. The State Labour Inspection, remember, for many years it has already controlled issues concerning the legality of employment, as I have also said today in the context of the 2020 report, but the scale we are dealing with at this point in time, the scale of the influx of refugees, is definitely disproportionate to what we have had in previous years. At this moment from the data collected on the portal work. gov.pl shows that in Poland it was registered in the system as 90,000 employees. workers who have taken up employment under new rules, already in force since they crossed the border in connection with the war in Ukraine. 71% These are women, 74% hired on the basis of civil-law contracts. Most of this is employment. with simple work, some found employment in processing, some through temporary employment agencies. These are preliminary data. On the other hand, as regards how the State Labour Inspection refers to this subject, firstly, we have developed a strategy aimed at long-term action to support this sector of the labour market and these workers, but also entrepreneurs. We've divided our actions into two stages. The first, the one that is happening at this moment, is connected with the finding of Ukrainian citizens here, in the Polish reality. This is mainly about the information and education campaign that we are sending to entrepreneurs so that they know how correctly, how to employ a worker from Ukraine in accordance with the regulations. And here we have webinars, podcasts, training in cooperation also with labour offices, with local actors who work in the labour market. And the second part of this information-prevention campaign concerning Ukrainian citizens is addressed directly to Ukrainian citizens through legal advice, which is also available in Ukrainian and Russian. Here, the Member of the Commission, among others: That's what she asked. In every district labour inspector we have the opportunity to provide advice, there is also a helpline for foreigners, but mainly for Ukrainian citizens. We have also posted on our website in a special panel addressed to Ukrainian citizens information not only on the provisions of the speciality, but also on the Act on the Promotion of Employment and Employment Market Institutions, as well as on how to move in the thicket of Polish law, translated into Ukrainian, also available in such a more visual version. Thus, here in fact, the labour inspection has prepared information materials that we provide to employees, but also interested persons can keep up to date with advice and information directly in the district labour inspectors and the Chief Labour Inspector in the Section of the National Labour Inspection Guidance Centre. On the other hand, on the basis of our experience, we also expect that the longer it lasts, the greater the likelihood that situations may arise in which the rights of workers from Ukraine will actually be violated. Therefore, we are also prepared to respond to such situations when requests for intervention, called complaints, will be made to us. We also have a procedure in place here that, if necessary, we will employ additional staff in our resources and jobs, who will be able to support us in the translation and control of this group of employees as well. I think that what is worth emphasising, because there were also such questions in this context, is the fact that workers who are foreigners have the right to the same treatment as workers, Polish citizens. This is clear from the provisions of the Labour Code: in the case of employment there is no possibility to differentiate the situation of workers due to, for example, nationality or other criteria, which are mentioned in c.p. Thus, in the case of employment on the basis of the employment relationship, the rights of workers of Ukraine must be similar to those of Polish citizens. I think it's in such a nutshell the most important information. The Authority has taken decisive action. From the beginning, right away, as soon as the situation happened, we were preparing. Here I address Mrs Mrzygłocka: the conference you mentioned was, among other things, a conference. on the preparation of the State Labour Inspection for the influx of an increased number of workers from Ukraine. On the other hand, there is also a question here about an issue that I am extremely interested in, which the labour inspection has really been on since the outbreak of the pandemic, but also in connection with the ongoing legislative work. Mr Sněžkou asked for remote work and what conditions should be met from the point of view of the State Labour Inspection so that the work can be carried out in a way that is in accordance with the rules, safe for workers. Legislative work on this issue is ongoing, they are taking place mainly in the Ministry of Family and Social Policy, and we, as an inspection, have also been very actively involved in it. What we are raising from the point of view of the State Labour Inspection is that there must be an aspect of health and safety at work at work even when it is done at home, because, as you have probably heard in the meantime, there are also accidents at work while working remotely. Unfortunately, these events were difficult, difficult for those workers who suffered an accident in their own home during remote work. In this respect, we also call for the legislation that is being made to cover the aspect of ensuring health and safety at work. This is extremely difficult because we move in space, which is the space of our own employee's apartment, and it requires a really new approach, one within which we train, inform, educate employees so as to prepare them for how to organize a workstation in a safe way. There was also a topic today - I look left-handed - that we have today's International Day of Remembrance for Victims of Accidents at Work and Occupational Diseases. I mention this because we really do not realize this, also with regard to remote work, how our behaviour affects how our health is later ruined, in short, because for example. We keep our laptop on our knees. The Labour Inspection calls for the inclusion in the scope of the regulation of remote work necessary regulations related to health and safety at work, including issues relating to mental health at work. The first studies that show up after remote work during the pandemic reveal additional psychosocial risks associated with remote work, which the labour inspection also draws attention to. This is in short, because the subject is actually extremely interesting. We assume that remote work will be a form that will remain with us, although it will be safer for employees to be a hybrid form, i.e. one that allows contact with a group of collaborators, social adaptation, support within a given workplace. What I would like to mention, perhaps not directly, answering the questions that you were also talking about, is that the labour inspection, like all areas involved in work, will have to face new challenges in the near future. Not only will it be remote work, but it will also be challenges related to artificial intelligence, technological change, work 4.0, transformation, which at the moment also occurs in the technological market, in the labour market. All of this affects the relationship between the employee and the employer and the labour inspection is already preparing for this at this point in time. Because it is not only a question of financial support, of staff support, but also answers to new challenges that we will all have to face because the labour market will change. As a result, we also participate as a State Labour Inspection in international work and labour inspection associations within the European Union. We will also hold a large international conference on how the State Labour Inspection, how inspections from different countries are to be found in a new reality. There's been a lot of talk about our preventive action, too, and I think that's the direction. For us, it's extremely important. I would very much like to thank all the committees and the Labour Protection Council here, but also the social partners who support us, who are involved in our preventive and promotional activities. With regard to the questions that have been asked in more detail, we will, of course, answer them in writing so that you can get all the answers to the questions you are interested in. ParlaMint-PL_2022-04-28-sejm-53-2.u387	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! Fire department in Piotrków Trybunalski. The plague of the old cities was fires, it was already seen in the Middle Ages. In Polish lands, it is considered as a precursor, and even as a creator of fire-fighting, that of Wolborz, the famous Renaissance publicist Andrzej Frycz Modrzewski. He created the first fire regulations and the first fire department unit in the 16th century in Wolborz, but the first professional firefighters were created in the Polish lands in the first half of the 19th century. It started with Warsaw and later was Kraków and Poznań. The fires were particularly strongly touched by Piotrków Trybunalski. Its stories are known from 1516, 1530, 1615, 1731. The red chicken can be exchanged for a long time. In 1865 the fire destroyed more than half of the city. Even this did not convince the Russian authorities at that time to create a city-style fire department. However, the Piotrków did not give up their efforts. In March 1873, however, a voluntary fire guard of the city of Piotrków was established, with 152 active members. The warden was Władysław Rozmarowski. The branch developed systematically, quickly gaining full uniform and necessary equipment. Piotrkowski firefighters practiced in a place where today there is a court in Salt Square. They wrote praiseworthy cards, as an example of a patriotic, civic attitude, as the researcher writes on this subject, Mariusz Nebula. Firefighters served in different structures after regaining independence and during the German occupation. After it was completed in 1951, a decision was taken on the construction of a new guard, which was completed after 4 years. It is necessary to write that the contemporary Piotrkowska guard is in a straight line a continuation of this one more than 100 years ago. Since the creation of separate legal acts of the State Fire Service in 1992, he participates in more than 800 shares a year. It is worth noting that in connection with the imposition of new obligations on firefighters, the actions include not only the elimination of fires, but also the elimination of other threats. I hope our guards need it as little as possible. On the occasion of the upcoming International Firefighter's Day, 4 May, I would like to present especially firefighters from Piotrków Trybunalski, Piotrkowski's, Belchatowski's, Tomaszowski's, and all firefighters of the State Fire Guard, voluntary firefighters' best wishes. So many trips, so many returns. Thank you. [[Applause]]
